First, let me establish my view of the theory's key sentences and how they apply.

Key theory claims:
- Part V: Account = (F1)∧(F2)∧(A)∧NonCircular∧NonVacuous. "the bell does not explain the tide" — literally covers O2.
- Part V: "A table of observed answers has no component whose relation is replaced by an intervention; it fails (F1) under any contract containing one. A table that genuinely encodes an organization's response to every admitted change is not a table in that sense: it satisfies (F1) as a decomposition does, and it is an account."
- Part III: "An account at a stated scope answers the question asked at that scope; it does not answer a broader question that failed, and a narrowing adopted after a failure is a new claim at a new index (Part VIII)." and "What makes a restriction appropriate to the question asked is a substantive, criticizable part of the claim; the semantics records the restriction and supplies no rule that certifies it."
- Part II: kinds are edit-signatures; measurement's signature vs causal assignment's.
- Part III: "A measure that identifies an outcome, with a reliable prediction from it, answers the identification question; whether the measured part also produces the outcome is the production question, and the first answer is not the second."
- Part X: Build, New, Origin; "A small binding newly prepared inside received content is construction of that binding, and the rest of the content keeps its inherited provenance."; "Reconstruction by a learner is construction; relay is not."; "A first representation may be constructed from an available problem without prior observation of what it represents."; Ownership: "Work supplied from outside that boundary... remains an outside contribution however it is executed inside; a process that runs inside the boundary is the system's own today whoever wrote it; and where the boundary is drawn decides, not where the process sits in the casing."
- Part XI: "A correct account that produced nothing, an act that repaired without an account, and a repair produced through use of an account are three different attributions."; ProducedBy clause; "The obligations are declared inputs... a protected condition is lost exactly when it fails on an occasion it covers."; "Losses outside P must be exposed."; "Repairing an obligation establishes... nothing about worth"; N as declared input.
- Part IX: active route definition: "A route that started and did no work, or that was already at rest when the result occurred, is not active for that result; whether a route is active is read from the history, not from the result."; receipts; "A record reconstructed from the claim it is meant to support is not a receipt for that claim."
- Part XII: "A replaced part that preserves the declared continuity leaves the same system..."; "Ownership is grounded in the processes and resources the boundary includes, never in the capability being attributed: 'owned because it can, and can because owned' grounds neither."; "The population is the set of transports the physics and the stated construction admit; a transport that would need a part every member of the population is built without is not in it."
- Part VIII: "A declared, invertible recoding of a carrier preserves the content when a reader who applies the declared convention recovers every pairing (Derivation 8)."; "A section of a carrier filled from another source keeps that other source's history, whatever it happens to match."; "A proposition indexed to a contract remains that proposition when a later theory changes the current contract. A new index is a new claim."
- Part VI: criticality relative to support; "the supports assessed are the ones actually written, not a support someone could write in their place."; "A route already present in the candidate is a route whether or not anyone has described its work; a component reassigned to a new target after a deletion belongs to a new candidate with its own assessment, and the new candidate's success is not the old one's."; interference.
- Part XIV: "The order is well founded: a representation justified only by its own construction... and a separate proof that would supply it counts only when the account uses it."; declared inputs.
- Part XVI Derivations 2, 3.

OK, let me settle the SPLIT-vs-SILENT distinction once and for all:
- SPLIT: the theory's text itself, read on the case as given, supports two verdicts (e.g., two sentences in tension, or an ambiguous term).
- SILENT: the theory's text is clear but needs an input/index the case doesn't provide, or stops short.
